Whole-project quality review of reify against dark-factory `docs/code-quality.md` (embedded in every prompt as the `code_quality.py::guidance` render; reify carries no copy). Phases 3–4: 30 area seats (opus/high), one blinded skeptic each (sonnet/high), 4 cross-area lenses (opus/high), synthesis and critic (fable/high). Slicing: src clusters packed to 40k src lines; each seat reads ~15k lines fully ranked by the instruments (never by line count) and indexes the rest; tests attached to the slice holding their crate; the infra suite counts as factory-infra code; the one-file `assemblies` area was read inside a language-pipeline slice with its anchors carrying area `assemblies`. The synthesis and critic that this report carries are the phase-4 re-run (wf_a67e6013-da0) with the 2026-10-11 prompts: short ids, critic severity_to and critic_responses, Calibrate phase. The first pass's synthesis (26 streams, 15 critic join errors, one revision) was superseded and is kept in the run scratch for comparison. Also folded in before synthesis: 15 findings (3 high) that the pipeline dropped for anchor shape (Rust `Type::method` anchors), recovered from the journal, anchors normalised to `Type.method`, re-verified by fresh skeptics in the Calibrate phase.   Read coverage, measured from the seats' own read_coverage against the snapshot: the 30 area seats were handed read lists totalling 298 src files / 427k lines (15k per seat) and self-reported 234k lines read (27% of the 880k src lines; 166 files read fully, many large files read in their production half and reported as indexed because the old schema had no `partial`). Of the 60 most fix-dense src files, 39 were in a read list, 31 were named by a seat as read or indexed, and 44 carry at least one finding; of the 16 files whose per-function CCN max is 60 or more, 9 were in a read list, 12 were named by a seat, none was reported read in full. Phase 2 was not run: 105 of 351 findings still cite fix-history (from the lead's git-log leads and the seats' own git use), but co-fix coupling, task-tracker bug-class recurrence and postmortem mining — the hotspot lane's distinctive evidence — were not produced, and the does-it-run lane (/review) was not assessed beyond the merge gate's continuous suite. Neither gap heals by itself: both siblings have no contract-shaped baseline and no trigger chain on reify, so the next /review-all's freshness table will show them stale again until each is run whole once; and because read lists are ranked by instruments that change slowly, index-only files tend to stay index-only across runs unless the next run's generator demotes files read fully this time (read_coverage in this record is the input for that). This rendering is over report-format's ~60 KB cap because the run carries 54 high findings rendered in full as the format requires; the JSON record is the source.

## Area × heuristic view

Ranking rule (from the synthesis, stated once): Payoff = severity x breadth x verdict, one rule applied to every ranked entry and never overridden by seat order: severity high=3, medium=2, low=1; breadth 1 when every measured site is inside the anchor's file, 2 when the measurements name several files inside one crate or one surface (one of: a crate, scripts/, tests/infra, gui/src-tauri, gui/src), 3 when they cross crates or surfaces (Rust+.ri, Rust+TS, bash+Python, docs+code); verdict confirmed=1, weakened=0.5, unverified=0.5 (the recovered engine_constraints/occt/engine_eval/engine_demand findings whose skeptic was lost to the usage cap, and p20, which no seat re-checked and which observation.unchecked should list). Breadth is read off each finding's statement and measurements by one script (synth2/rank.py: distinct crate paths, crate names and basenames resolved against git ls-files), with hand corrections where the text names modules without a file suffix (f263 15 files read Engine fields -> 2; f210 SingleKernelHolder in reify-geometry vs the trait in reify-ir -> 3; f69 four tokenizers in three reify-eval files -> 2; f2 elastic_static+modal_ops -> 2; f180 loop_closure+snapshot -> 2; f111 test file + harness-layout-lib private names -> 2; f137 verify.sh text pinned by 56 tests/infra members -> 3; f285 lib.rs summary parsed in ptodo/pdoccover/pdcheck/pcite -> 2; f70 handle.rs+lib.rs -> 2 as the critic measured; f324 24 modules of one crate -> 2) and for priors read off their statements (p2 two crates -> 3; p3 engine.rs+commands -> 2; p4 debug_server+AppState -> 2; p17 cache.rs+record_evaluation callers -> 2; p18 CLI/GUI/LSP entry points -> 3; p20 repo-wide -> 3). Ties keep seat order (ascending id). A prior re-checked this run takes this run's verdict: still_present read as confirmed (p2, p7, p8, p12, p13, p14), 'narrowed'/'partly fixed' read as weakened (p3, p4, p10, p17, p18); the seven priors observation.fixed lists (p1, p5, p6, p9, p11, p15, p19) are not ranked; p16 is not ranked because it is deferred under #5023 (see contradictions) and sits in S3 only as that milestone's obligation. Severities changed from the record on the critic's evidence: f324, f139, f70 and f240 to medium (f240/f206/f196/f277 are one glob-namespace mechanism, all medium, so f263 leads language-pipeline|h13). Area relabels by slices.json ownership: f186 -> kinematics-and-motion, f319 -> analysis, f326 -> language-pipeline, f268 -> tooling, f324 -> constraints-and-geometry. Duplicated ids across seats were kept once at the higher severity x verdict (f196 medium, f229 high, f237 high confirmed, f228 low). Rows exist only for area x primary tag pairs that have findings (87 rows, 360 ranked entries); h14 rows hold only filed h14 findings (f8, f43, f48, f105, f108, f147), while every seat-measured stays_whole:false file whose production region passes the 2,000-line alarm is recorded as an open h14 measurement in the stream that performs the split and listed under exonerations as not exonerated. Mechanical one-anchor findings the lead files without a stream: f22, f89, f136, f189, f208, f251, f261, f262, f270. Critic fixes applied this revision: rows recomputed from the formula (analysis|h11 now f13 f26 f30 f40 f319 at 6 each, f24 at 3; factory-infra|h11 f103 at 9 first, f125 6, f139 2); p16 removed from language-pipeline|h6; the h14 exoneration narrowed to stays_whole:true files plus qoi.rs's test-module move, with every stays_whole:false production half above the alarm named against its stream and no test-share figure used anywhere; five area relabels; four severity changes; p20 at 0.5; S20 deletable recounted to the four acquire-side derivations plus the FD 8/9 split with lock-guard:197 and audit:1336 as callers of lane_lock_path; S21 stated as extending lib_gui_launch.sh with the deletable recounted (run-with-occt.sh's two prepends, run-gui.sh's snap stanza, verify.sh's export); S16 deletes morph() plus its one stub-pin test and re-drives the three eligibility tests; S5 shares only the f64 slab codec and the severity pair after the contract crate's dependency flips; S12 states the Build-then-check coupling with the CLI heavy path as the single protocol site; S10 derives KNOWN_V02_KERNELS from KernelId with a test instead of deleting it; S4's open choice states the structural benefit, not a line count. Nothing is ranked or justified by raw line count, average complexity, coverage or test count; the line figures quoted in stream text are seat measurements of where a split lands, not payoff inputs.

## High findings

### factory-infra

#### new (10)

- **fk-1417bf006761** `f93` `tests-infra-3.1` — `tests/infra/test_slot_timeout_marker.sh` · h12, h6, h9, h14, comments · **high** · ✓ confirmed · structural · `open`
  - The file's subject is the slot-timeout emission contract: Sections A, B, C and H, about 804 lines and 67 asserts. Sections D, E, F and G are 3,413 of its 4,384 lines (78%) and 76 asserts. They implement a line-oriented regex static analyzer over every tests/infra test_*.sh plus its .py sibling, made of 29 named F_*/G_* ERE constants, a worklist fixed-point invocation closure, an exec-forwarding-lib rule, bash and Python diversion dialects, and a two-pass awk block scanner. It is maintained by hand through a 12-entry D_ROSTER and a 9-row, three-array, index-aligned G_MEMBERS/G_SITE/G_SCAN table. The analyzer exists only so that no infra test's stderr carries the column-0 string `@@REIFY_SLOT_TIMEOUT@@`, because dark-factory classifies a verify by grepping log text for it. - the fused-memory loader takes no date and always pulls the whole corpus;
- the only reader of `ctx.window` is P2's unbounded-backlog guard, which tests `window.is_none()`. Passing `--since` therefore disables that guard while leaving the backlog unbounded, as p2_consumer_stub.rs's own KNOWN LIMITATION comment admits.

- **fk-88643a4cc993** `f341` `stateless.1` — `crates/reify-eval/src/engine_constraints.rs::check` · h7, h10, h5 · **high** · ~ weakened *(weakened: Read engine_constraints.rs check() rustdoc (2325-2390), dispatch_constraints, unmeasured_reason (880-922), representation_within_indeterminate_reason (925-935),)* · structural · `open`
  - Engine::check's verdicts for three constraint families depend on which other public Engine calls ran before it, not on its arguments.

Three check-time passes read Engine side tables that only other public surfaces populate:
- dispatch_constraints reads achieved_repr_tol. That table is written only by tessellate_realizations or tessellate_snapshot, and only after set_capture_repr_tol(true). An empty table turns every RepresentationWithin into Indeterminate.
- measure_dfm_rules and measure_gdt_conformance inject kernel handles from realization_handles. That table is written only by the Build/BuildSnapshot surfaces and cleared only at the next Build entry.

check's rustdoc states the required sequence: callers 'must call set_capture_repr_tol(true) followed by tessellate_realizations(&compiled) before calling check()', and DFM needs a subject 'realized by a prior build() call'. The sequence…
  - check-time passes reading side tables written by other public calls: 3 (dispatch_constraints ← achieved_repr_tol; measure_dfm_rules, measure_gdt_conformance ← realization_handles) *(rule: functions enclosing self.achieved_repr_tol / self.realization_handles reads in engine_constraints.rs (lines 527, 1837, 2)*
  - production check() call sites that perform the documented pre-call protocol: 1 of 6 (cli main.rs:997 after set_capture_repr_tol + realize_for_check + tessellate_realizations; not: main.rs:1001, lsp analysis.rs:183, lsp diagnostics.rs:355 *(rule: git grep of `.check(&` in crates/reify-cli/src, crates/reify-lsp/src, gui/src-tauri/src excluding tests and #[cfg(test)])*
  - writers vs clearers of realization_handles: written at 3 Build-surface sites in engine_build.rs; cleared only by reset_per_build_state on Build/BuildSnapshot; not cleared by reset_geometry_for_reload (whi *(rule: git grep self.realization_handles in crates/reify-eval/src plus read of reset_geometry_for_reload body (engine_admin.rs )*
  - next change: The plausible next change is one of three: add RepresentationWithin or DFM support to the LSP or GUI, add a new measured constraint family, or move the GUI reset or tessellate relative to check. Doing it correctly requires knowing that check's answers are only as good as the call sequence that preceded it. That sequenc
  - proposal: Make the measurement an input. Either have check take (or build itself, behind a flag) a MeasurementContext holding achieved_repr_tol and the realization→handle map produced by an explicit realize/tessellate step, or expose a single check_with_geometry(module, CheckGeometry) entry that runs realize → tessellate → check

## All findings

One line each: key, anchor, severity letter (* = critic correction or dispute, see JSON), verdict glyph (✓ confirmed, ~ weakened, ? unverified), class letter (s structural, m mechanical), primary tag; grouped new / re-observed / standing / fixed / unchecked. Everything else is in the JSON record.

### factory-infra

**factory-infra/tests-infra-3** — The three Python members (test_review_readme.py, test_rust_production_view_lib.py, test_reify_audit_pprdstatus_wiring.py) are healthy. They drive the real script as a subprocess against hermetic tempdir fixtures, assert only on outcomes, put prose under 3%, and keep each fixture seam in one run_script / run_lexer helper. They are the model the migration policy points at. The cost sits in the four bash size-alarm files, which hold 16,301 of the slice's lines.

Three shapes repeat across them:
(1) Most of their volume is static analysis of other files' source, or prose aimed at a reviewer, not behavioural tests of their subject. test_slot_timeout_marker.sh spends 78% of its lines on a regex call-graph analyzer over tests/infra. test_test_helpers.sh spends 750 lines pinning other files' source shape. Prose shares run 41-56%, with 77 "MEASURED" paragraphs in one file and 334 file:line citations repo-wide in tests/infra, and most of the citations I sampled are stale.
(2) Invariants that hold across the whole suite are enforced at one position in the file, or by copy-transposition, instead of by one mechanism. This shows up as the seed suite's mid-file isolation detectors, the PTODO scenarios (d)-(g) with five schema copies and dead sentinels, and test_run_all.sh's 233 mirrored skip-asserts.
(3) The real interfaces are text-log protocols: 28 distinct @@TOKEN@@ families plus byte-exact Summary/FAILED lines that dark-factory and sibling meta-tests grep. Every guard therefore becomes a grep, and every new emitter or consumer becomes a parser.

The next-change cost is concrete. A new infra test that spawns run_all.sh turns the whole pool red until someone edits four index-aligned arrays in an unrelated file (commit 539851112c). Porting one member to Python took seven commits to teach the analyzer a Python dialect.

**factory-infra/tests-infra-2-build-utils** — The slice has two sides. reify-build-utils is a small crate that does one thing: a closed NativeDep enum, one find() core with an injectable override seam, and RAII-tempdir tests that re-exec the test binary instead of calling env::set_var. The next-change cost there is not inside lib.rs. It sits at its two outward seams. (a) Its discovery data has a hand-kept bash mirror in check-manifold-deps.sh, and the only thing holding the two together is awk parsers over the Rust source text. (b) Every consumer build.rs re-derives the has_<dep> cfg, the check-cfg declaration and the env-rerun declarations by hand. The Python members are healthy: test_hooks_armed_guard.py (a real git oracle next to every guard verdict, no source greps), test_lane_lock_probe.py (real flock contention, a tri-state check on every observation) and their thin .sh wrappers are the shape the CLAUDE.md migration policy asks for. test_refresh_warm_base.sh, test_jcodemunch_index_reify.sh and the nextest_absent lib/test pair are also sound: a lib plus a test, PATH stubs, behaviour checked through exit codes and argv. Most of their weight is defensive prose. The cost sits in the large bash gate files: (1) a test that re-implements verify.sh's role/wall semantics by scraping its source; (2) a file named for the OCCT flock wrapper that has become the home of verify.sh timeout-knob contracts; (3) a 2,645-line guard that keeps its engine, its self-tests and its live gate in one file, and most of its prose answers reviewers. Fix and amend commits run 2-13 per file since 2026-07-06. They cluster exactly on the files that parse or mirror another file's text.

**factory-infra/tests-infra-1-cargo** — The slice has two tiers. The Python tier is healthy: the seven unittest files I sampled are stdlib-only, have very little prose (under 7%), drive public seams (a reader Protocol, main(connect=...), or a black-box subprocess), and patch nothing and reach no private names. The one fixture that routes on query text says so in its docstring. The bash tier is where the next change gets expensive. The four largest bash files are 50-57% comment lines. Each fuses several purposes: run_all.sh is the discovery runner, four separately written execution modes, a content-skip engine, a flaky ledger and the signal handling, all in one 2104-line file. test_harness_kloc_cap.sh holds the guard's implementation (about 790 lines of rule functions), the repo's normative harness-layout contract (35 crates/**/tests files point to it) and its own self-tests. Fix history shows where the cost lands. run_all.sh's cross-cutting features were added once per mode, and the gaps were closed by later follow-up commits. kloc_cap took 84 commits since 2026-07-06, 18 of them prose corrections. Every file:line citation I checked in the bash tier is stale (16 of 16), while a Python docstring's cite into run_all.sh is still correct today. Shared policy is copied across files instead of living in one place: the run_all discovery predicate has 4 copies, the slowdown bound has 2 with different semantics, and the OCCT LD_LIBRARY_PATH policy has 4. No mechanism keeps those copies in step except prose and a few pinned tests. .cargo is two small files; the runner's rationale comment is false today.

**factory-infra/tests-infra-4** — This slice is the infra self-tests for verify.sh's scope picker, the tree-sitter build pipeline, and the warm-lane gc/pool/audit scripts. They are behavioural: real git fixtures, real processes and flocks, causal handshakes instead of sleeps, and vacuity guards before negative assertions. The Python port (test_verify_env_ambient_isolation.py) is healthy. Its ad-hoc verify_env reader agrees with a real YAML load on all 11 keys at this tree, it drives one spawn funnel, and its 27 tests match the wrapper's claim. The next-change cost sits in five places. (1) The only window onto verify.sh's decisions is its human-readable --print-plan text. Tests grep ordered substrings of a prose header and regexes of shell command text, so the field order of a comment line is a cross-file contract. (2) Test-side copies of production predicates or inventories drift silently: the e2e suite's tree-sitter readiness check, the hand-maintained verify.sh source-closure copy lists, and the warm-lane-audit fixtures that restate dark-factory's record byte shapes. (3) test_tree_sitter_pipeline.sh runs a private harness whose failure lines run_all.sh's FAIL-detail extractor cannot see. (4) Navigation prose has rotted: 27 of 32 file:line cites point at the wrong code, the block indexes in file headers omit most blocks, and 24 scenario banners print a stale 'RED until step-N' into every green log. (5) Within files, per-block fixtures are copied out instead of factored into helpers. All seven size alarms are mostly scenario catalogues around one SUT. Each splits cleanly once its fixture helpers move to a downward lib. The only real heuristic-13 symptom is test_verify_scope.sh's GV-6, which reads PG-DRIFT's derived variables. pipe-to-grep assertions (107 in test_verify_scope.sh) bypass plan_capture_lib's fork-free predicates, but the flaky ledger records only 2 flakes for that member, so this is context, not a finding.

**factory-infra/scripts-1-hooks** — Most of this slice is in good shape. The newer Python tools take typed values at their boundaries and keep one owner per concern: docs-truth-sweep.py, pprdstatus-escalate.py, escalation_mcp.py, reify_audit_findings.py, cargo-test-tally.py, the graph-task-conflation-census.py + task_reference_grammar.py pair (enums, frozen dataclasses, a Protocol-typed reader), and await-merge-landing.py's verdict enum. affected-crates-lib.sh is well factored. The hooks put the six enforce/bypass switches behind one gate_switch_on, which is heuristic 10 done right. jcodemunch-index-reify.sh shows the right way to handle a duplicated derivation: it has three copies of the repo-id formula, and a Rust test holds them in parity. The next-change cost sits in three places. (1) The PRD probe gate (prd-capability-check.py / prd-decompose-verify.py / .mjs, 40 commits since 2026-07-06). Its outcomes travel as strings: sentinels injected into stderr, ENOENT regexes, and verdict and disposition vocabularies spelled separately in Python, the JSON schema and prompt prose. 10 of those 40 commits re-anchor a stderr match. One check-kind vacuity is live today: a 'resolves' premise with an empty match PASSes when `reify check` fails. (2) Policies kept in parallel copies with nothing checking the copies against each other: path-to-crate classification (bash, its Python 'mirror' and verify.sh), the MCP client, and the verify-pipeline set rebuilt by regex-parsing verify.sh. Each has already drifted at least once. (3) The heavily commented shell primitives, seed-warm-lane.sh (61% comments, 34 fix-grep'd commits) and verify-pipeline-guard.sh (79%). Their prose is largely written to answer a reviewer: measurements, amendment histories, defences of where code sits, and file:line pointers that have since moved. 16 of the 58 commits to seed-warm-lane.sh since 07-06 touched only comments. Neither size-alarmed test file has a heuristic-13 obstacle to splitting. test_prd_decompose_verify.py carries about 1575 lines that test a different file (the .mjs).

**factory-infra/scripts-2** — The slice is two different things. The warm-lane family (gc, gc-sweep, audit, lock-guard, disk-guard, preflight, ref-check, degenerate-ref-check, source-integrity) is small single-purpose CLIs. Each has a clear stdout/exit contract, fail-direction invariants (A1-A6) that are stated and pinned, and three correctly extracted shared seams (lib_live_refs.sh, lib_lane_lock.sh, lib_task_citation.sh). lock-guard, disk-guard, ref-check, degenerate-ref-check and source-integrity are healthy as written. with-jcodemunch-serve.sh is also healthy: one lifecycle, with teardown driven by observed outcomes. Its cost is prose: 65% of the file, much of it measurement records. In this family the next-change cost sits in helpers and contracts that were copied instead of shared (status oracle, glob matcher, branch-to-id parsing), one env var that names two different directories, hand-rolled JSON in audit, and gc's 337-line _do_reclaim. verify.sh is the real hotspot: 4682 lines, 69% comment lines, 223 task-number mentions, 179 commits since 2026-07-06 (86 fix/amend). Its cost is structural. The plan is a list of shell strings that 56 infra test files pin as text. The DF_VERIFY_ROLE axis is spread over 16 inline decision sites plus two exemptions in other files. Six facts owned elsewhere are copied as hand-maintained lists. Every phase talks to the next through global variables, so any split by sourcing would be stitching. Lead verdict ('a green run exits red', 2026-06-09): today's protections are per-site and documented (_verify_cleanup ends on `true`, the node-lane trap uses `if kill ...; then :; fi`, a once-only ledger guard). I found no structural guard, such as an executed-plan test asserting rc=0 with the overlapped node lane. I did not prove that no such test exists, so this is not raised as a finding. The priority order is F1 (structured plan steps), then F2 (one role-policy table), then F3 (derive the coupled-fixture sets).

### language-pipeline

**language-pipeline/core-eval-engine-build-compiler-type-compat-expr-kleene** — The 2026-07 hardening wave really did land the two mechanisms the prior findings asked for. RealizationOpsInput/RealizationOutputs replaced the ~24-parameter signature. probe_realization_cache was pulled out and has five tests, including a cold/warm side-effect parity test. allocate_snapshot_version is now the single version allocator, with a lint-style gate in version_id_discipline_gate.rs. reset_per_build_state destructures Engine exhaustively, so a new field won't compile until it is classified. type_compat.rs is in good shape: small pure predicates, infer_mul_div_result as the single source checked against the runtime truth table, and the overload tiers delegated to reify_core::overload. Every pub(crate) item its tests use also has a production consumer. kleene.rs is a correct single source for the truth tables. The cost of the next change now sits in three places. (1) engine_build.rs execute_realization_ops kept growing after the extraction: 2,317 lines, nested 16 levels deep, 34 task ids cited. (2) Four realization-loop drivers hand-copy the per-template walk, so each new per-realization knob or write-back has to be decided four times. This has already left one recorded obligation open after its task closed (tessellate drops produced_repr even though the tessellate path now demands non-BRep reprs). (3) Prose: 49% of engine_build production lines, 91% of diagnostics.rs production lines, and a 305-line history-and-ruling module doc on top of ty.rs. Some of it has drifted (INV-BUILD-1 described as 'proposed', a cancelled #2947 cited as a blocker, line-number Origin pointers, a retired reify-types crate). One gap in reify-core/compiler: there is no shared Type traversal, so 8 exhaustive walkers stay in lock-step only by convention. reify-compiler's crate-root glob namespace (`use super::*` plus 28 `pub(crate) use X::*`) makes type_compat's imports opaque.

**language-pipeline/compiler-expr-compiler-compile-builder-expr-lib-compiler-entity-eval-bom** — The slice holds three of the repo's biggest single functions. compile_expr_guarded_with_expected_inner (reify-compiler expr.rs) is one 5,369-line match: the FunctionCall arm is 1,857 lines and MemberAccess is 922. compile_entity (entity.rs) is 3,800 lines and runs three passes that share function-local classification sets. eval_expr in reify-expr lib.rs is 868 lines and dispatches 23 named builtin arms plus 4 user-function families by bare string name. Most of the next-change cost comes from that string dispatch. The facts "which call is intercepted" and "what type does a geometry call have" have no typed home, so every consumer recovers them on its own: dual_eval re-lists eval_expr's intercepts, seven compiler sites look at the callee name to tell geometry apart, the skeleton path re-runs entity.rs's let classification, and reify-eval multi_case.rs re-implements the reify-expr solve_load_cases fallback. The comments describe each of these mirrors, but none of them is checked mechanically. All 21 lib.rs:NNN pointers in dual_eval.rs are stale, and so are the entity.rs:NNN pointers in the skeleton path. That is drift the tests cannot catch. Some places are healthy. The MemberAccess type dispatch is now an exhaustive no-wildcard match over all 39 Type variants. The poison helpers (make_poison_literal / propagate_poison) put one invariant in one place. find_matching_compiled_function delegates to the shared tier predicates in reify_core::overload. The tests_reaching_internals rows for compile_builder/* are false positives: the phase functions are pub(crate) and production lib.rs calls them, while the integration tests only name them in comments. The 170 tests embedded in reify-expr lib.rs drive the public eval_expr seam. reify-compiler is a flat set of peer modules: lib.rs glob re-exports 30 modules and 27 modules `use super::*`. Neither expr.rs nor entity.rs can be read on its own. I measured the 22 test-file size alarms only by line counts, prose share and test-name clusters. I did not read them.

**language-pipeline/ir-value-ir-geometry-compiler-conformance-eval-demand** — The slice is four files, each over the 2,000-line alarm. geometry.rs (13,364 lines) holds the whole kernel seam in one module: handle ids, the repr and capability classifiers, the GeometryOp IR plus its descriptor table, the GeometryQuery IR, the mesh contract, the STL/3MF writers, the error types, the 27-method GeometryKernel trait and persistent naming. Its production region is 59% comment lines, and 52% of the file is an embedded test module. conformance/mod.rs (8,753) is a compile-time conformance walker whose production region is 60% comments, with 69% of the file embedded tests. checker.rs holds the ten-phase trait-conformance pipeline. value.rs is mostly tests (67%). demand.rs is small and healthy.

The 2026-07 hotspot program fixed both prior findings in their headline form. Mesh now has a typed validator. The topology attribute table is keyed by KernelHandle. In both cases the enforcement still depends on convention rather than types, and that is where the next change costs the most:
- The GeometryKernel trait grows by adding defaulted capability methods. Delegating wrappers have to forward each one by hand, and they have stopped doing so: SingleKernelHolder forwards 18 of 27.
- The ValidatedMesh witness exists, but no consumer signature requires it.
- Kernel replies (GeometryQuery returns Value) and op parameters (GeometryOp fields are Value in 27 of the 37 variants that carry numbers) cross the seam as untyped Value. Kind vocabularies are re-parsed from strings on the eval side.

In the compiler, the cost is mostly prose and parallel rule tables. About a third of the recent commits on conformance/mod.rs are prose maintenance. The phantom-default guards live as four parallel skip sets checked in two phases. One walker re-encodes compatibility rules that type_compatible already owns, and one function re-encodes the joint-constructor table. The tests pin the phase decomposition rather than behaviour.

**language-pipeline/syntax-ts-parser-eval-engine-edit-compiler-type-resolution-compiler-geom** — This slice is five size-alarm files. The parser and the persistent cache are in reasonable shape. The cost sits in two places: the eval engine's edit transaction, and the compiler's type and geometry dispatch.

Healthy parts:
- ts_parser.rs is one cohesive CST-to-AST `impl Lowering`: 135 methods, an approximate CCN max of about 48 (lower_source_file), and no reach-back imports.
- Its child fault_diagnosis.rs is a clean downward split, as is persistent_cache/diagnostics_wire.rs.
- The persistent_cache GC and IO code is linear, with the races documented.

The costly part: engine_edit.rs holds the warm edit transaction twice. edit_param runs 1938 lines and edit_source 1682 lines; together they are about 75% of the file's production code, and production code is about 50% prose. edit_source still has no production caller. It is a hand copy that has drifted from edit_param in at least four places. Since 2026-07-06, 14 commits for tasks 4729, 6373, 6423, 6998 and 7114 went into keeping it in parity.

INV-EVAL enforcement found today:
- INV-EVAL-1: a type (the commit_cell_result primitive with CacheLeg), but it is opt-in. Bare values-plus-snapshot inserts remain in reelaborate_guarded_group and in edit_source's Phase 1, post-wave2 and collection-resize phases.
- INV-EVAL-2: a type (cell_eval_ctx), also opt-in. eval_ctx_with_meta is still reachable crate-wide; edit_source uses it at 7 cell-eval sites, 4 of them without determinacy. The dead Engine::cell_eval_ctx method is kept with expect(dead_code).
- INV-EVAL-3: partial (store_cell_replay_diagnostics on edit_param's main walk).
- INV-EVAL-5: run_unified_pass_seeded is the one scheduler on both edit paths.
- INV-EVAL-6: intent only (TODO #5023).

In reify-compiler:
- type_resolution.rs passes three resolution rules through thread-local ambient sets.
- It writes the parameterized-builtin table three times across two resolvers that have already diverged.
- geometry.rs sends 60 builtin names through one 1581-line match, and the same names are restated as string tables in at least five files.
- Both files open with a `use super::*` glob into the crate root.

**language-pipeline/test-support-eval-engine-eval-compiler-geometry-modify-spec-conformance** — engine_eval.rs (15,306 lines, 41% prose, 2,922 lines of embedded tests) is still the evaluator's centre of gravity. It holds Engine::eval (about 2,000 lines) and Engine::eval_cached (about 1,530 lines). It also holds two Let-cell evaluators that are 77% textually identical, the solver-problem builders, nine post-pass detectors, sampled-field config parsing and undef-cause classification. The 2026-07 hardening wave landed real primitives: commit_cell_result/CacheLeg, the required-args cell_eval_ctx, DetectorRegistry, and catch_unwind at the compute trampoline. But it migrated only some call sites onto them. The raw paths stay callable and dominate: 22 raw eval_ctx_with_meta sites against 3 cell_eval_ctx sites in this file, 29 inline journal.record literals, 1 registered detector against 9 hand-listed ones. So the next change still costs one decision per eval path (cold eval, cold merged, warm eval_cached, warm merged, edit_param, edit_source). The paths already disagree on solve diagnostics, context capabilities, panic recovery and keyed-sub validation. Prose escalates to fill the gap: it documents divergences, review rounds and line coordinates instead of removing them. reify-test-support is organised into many small cohesive modules. Its three alarmed files are large mostly because of embedded tests (2,800, 2,077 and 1,319 lines) and prose; each partitions cleanly. MockGeometryKernel (1,306 references in 132 files) is still outside the shared kernel contract. reify-spec-conformance is a 45-line prose-only stub awaiting leaf gamma, which is healthy for its stage. geometry_modify.rs is small but encodes its op arguments as string keys that the evaluator re-parses.

**language-pipeline/eval-freshness-walk-compiler-member-path-expr-field-reductions-ir-constr** — The slice is a set of individually well-built leaf modules sitting in a compiler crate that has no internal layering. For the 11 files marked read-fully, I read the production half in full. Their embedded #[cfg(test)] modules I only skimmed, and engine_hash_algo, arg_acceptance and forall_elaborate I read in part. ri_literal, geometry_list and member_path's hop resolver each have narrow typed interfaces, structured errors and exhaustive matches (ri_literal's RiLiteralError and UnitScope are good examples). engine_hash_algo's coverage table is enforced by tests (engine_hash_tests, test_engine_hash_closure.sh), and only three persisted targets exist, so the hand-kept PERSISTED_TARGET_SOURCES gap is contained.

The next-change cost sits in five places.
(1) Ambient names in reify-compiler. lib.rs glob-re-exports 28 modules, and 25 top-level files `use super::*`, so peer modules call each other unqualified. One example is the entity.rs<->guards.rs cycle. CompilationScope is a 30-field pub(crate) bag that 11 files read and write directly.
(2) Invariants the hotspot program registered as 'type'-enforced are opt-in primitives, not gates. INV-EVAL-1: commit_cell_result exists, but at least 7 undocumented hand-rolled four-leg value-cell commits remain in engine_eval.rs, and CacheStore::record_evaluation is `pub`. INV-EVAL-3: DetectorRegistry holds one detector, the annotation post-pass is ordered by call position at two sites, and edit_check never runs it.
(3) Meaningful strings crossing compiler->IR->eval: reflective query_kind, forall-connect port templates rewritten with replacen, and the journal `|cache-skip=` payload parsed with contains().
(4) Parallel authorities: member_path's chain walk versus expr.rs's live member matchers; kernel_validation versus arg_acceptance (same crate) for dimension classification and wording; and four hand-written MemberDecl dispatch loops that each decide support per member kind.
(5) Prose doing a mechanism's job. stdlib_loader uses 242 comment lines and 14 MUST constraints instead of import edges. 41 file:line pointers across the 14 files are drifting; I verified three as already stale: kernel_validation's geometry_ops.rs:482 (now 615), detectors' engine_eval.rs:6312/4400-4557, and lib.rs:270-277. Several user-visible Info diagnostics in forall_elaborate cite done tasks 2629/2690/2717 as "future scope".

Lower-cost items I measured but did not file:
- guards.rs::collect_body_refs_inner is a 130-line copy of reify_ir's collect_value_refs_inner that differs in one arm (Lambda body vs captures), and its only consumer is expr.rs.
- forall_elaborate::fields_equal rebuilds Diagnostic equality by hand outside reify-core, so adding a field to Diagnostic silently weakens the dedupe.
- lib.rs has 11 compile_with_* entry points over three axes (prelude source x checker x auto config). Three of them have no consumer outside lib.rs (compile_with_stdlib_with_config, compile_with_prelude_checked, compile_with_prelude_refs_checked).

Leads, with today's mechanism for each:
- INV-EVAL-1: primitive plus unit tests. It is bypassable, so it is not a type gate.
- INV-EVAL-3: a registry wired into three paths, but the second post-pass sits outside it. That is test+partial, not 'one owner'.
- INV-EVAL-2/4/5/6: outside this slice.

**language-pipeline/eval-cache-eval-engine-admin-compiler-builtin-signatures-compiler-units** — The slice is reify-eval's cache, dependency and admin layer plus reify-compiler's builtin-name catalogue. Three structural facts drive what the next change costs. (1) Engine is a 67-field private struct whose `impl Engine` blocks are spread over 19 files. 63 of its fields are reached via `self.<field>` from two or more files. engine_admin.rs is the catch-all satellite: constructors, registries, 28 cfg(test-instrumentation) seams, a shell-GUI mesh decoder, and kernel-pin diagnostics. It also has a mis-attached rustdoc: the "Validate that `value` is a safe write" doc block sits on `module_has_auto_cells`, and `validate_param_override` has none. (2) Invalidation and dependency logic has several homes. There are three graph-to-dependency builders in deps.rs, and three dirty mechanisms (the one-hop CacheStore.dirty_reasons, dirty::compute_dirty_cone, and the dead cache::dirty_set), plus the still-unwired freshness walk (prior finding). (3) Builtin facts are spread across 16 name tables in a file called units.rs, an arg-slot table in builtin_signatures.rs whose production code is 73% comments, geometry.rs lowering and the eval chokepoint. The reify-builtins registry PRD (#6001) is the planned single home but has seeded only 2 families. Healthy parts: the CompiledExpr walkers in reify-ir are exhaustive with no `_` arms, so a new variant fails to compile everywhere it must be handled. unfold.rs layers cleanly (recursion and nesting call down into cell elaboration, no cycles). The `cell_commit` / `cell_eval_ctx` / `detectors` primitives exist and are adopted at most template-scope sites. The PTODO baseline went from 5 lines to 0 since 2026-08-11. INV-EVAL enforcement today: INV-EVAL-1 is a type primitive (32 production commit_cell_result sites), but 13 direct record_evaluation* production sites remain. INV-EVAL-2 has a required-args constructor at 11 sites, defeated at instance scope by a NoContainment object and a discarded diagnostics sink. INV-EVAL-3 stores diagnostics in NodeCache, but capture is an opt-in post-record setter at 5 sites, with 3 replay sites and a detector registry. INV-EVAL-4 is a test (cache_divergence audit). INV-EVAL-5 is a test (no_stale_undef gate). INV-EVAL-6 is intent only (TODO(#5023)). docs/invariants.md still marks all six 'proposed'.

**language-pipeline/eval-resolve-order-eval-lib-compiler-annotations-compute-contract-eval-g** — How I read it: I read the production code of reify-eval lib.rs (lines 1-1690) and dirty.rs (1-469) in full. I read most of the production code of graph.rs (1-700), engine_purposes.rs (16-1104), relation_signatures.rs (1-700), the geometry-kind enums in types.rs, and the instance-path code in resolve_order.rs. For the other size-alarm files I built a symbol index and checked the specific seams named below. Test modules were indexed, not read.

Structure today: the slice centres on one type. `Engine` is a 67-field struct defined in reify-eval's crate root. Its 770-line definition is 91% comments. 23 `impl Engine` blocks are spread over 18 files, and 15 files read its private fields directly via `self.<field>`. Because the struct sits at the crate root, every child module can see its private state, so these files are satellites over that state, not layers. The next change to anything stored on the engine has to know which of about 6 lifetime classes (per-build, per-eval, purpose, config, instrumentation, cross-edit cache) a field belongs to. That classification lives in prose in lib.rs and again in `engine_build.rs::reset_per_build_state`'s exhaustive destructure. The destructure is a real mechanism (INV-BUILD-1): it catches a field nobody classified, but not a field put in the wrong class. That function's own comments record exactly that kind of misclassification bug (#5415, relate_static_facts).

Second cost centre: identities built from strings and per-name tables. Instance paths are `format!`-ed strings that are parsed again by hand. Relation and purpose vocabularies are spelled out in parallel string-keyed `match` tables. The value-cell content-hash formula is rewritten inline at 8 sites.

Healthy parts: dirty.rs (the scheduling core is a single delegate, and the cycle-drop contract is stated where it belongs), resolve_order's algorithms, relation_signatures' tangent combo enum (one home for its ΔDOF), and the ConstraintCheckEntry verdict invariant (enforced at construction and at set_verdict).

INV-EVAL enforcement today (docs/invariants.md still lists all six, and INV-FEA-1, as "proposed"):
- INV-EVAL-1: the `commit_cell_result` primitive exists, with 25 production calls (engine_eval 14, engine_edit 8, unfold 3). Purpose let-injection writes `snapshot.values` directly. No lint.
- INV-EVAL-2: the required-args `cell_eval_ctx` exists, with 13 production calls, against 62 calls to the permissive `eval_ctx_with_meta`.
- INV-EVAL-3: the detector registry is called at 6 sites.
- INV-EVAL-4: `check_snapshot_cache_divergence` is only called from tests.
- INV-EVAL-5: one Kahn core (`engine_fixpoint::run_unified_pass_seeded`), plus `check_no_stale_undef` and a debug-only `assert_dag_complete_from_graph`.
- INV-EVAL-6: not checked.

**language-pipeline/compiler-orientation-signatures-eval-compute-cache-key-compiler-si-units** — The slice is a set of small, mostly single-purpose leaf modules (median under 400 lines, no file near the heuristic-14 alarm), and many of them are clean: fn_return_check.rs, indeterminate.rs, stdlib_topo.rs, prelude_context.rs, recursion_guard.rs and builtin_registry.rs each have one purpose, a narrow interface and invariants carried by types. The next-change cost is not inside these files. It sits in the seams between them and their siblings outside the slice. (1) The same fact lives in more than one table with no derivation between them: the datum-projection table exists three times (compiler typing, reify-expr eval, reify-eval self-datum) and has already drifted into a silent-Undef bug; grid analysis kernels are written twice; geometry op names are spelled in 5 to 8 files although reify-ir has a descriptor table. (2) Builtin result typing has two mechanisms at once: a 22-arm string ladder over per-family name-slice modules (orientation/joint/flexure/list_helpers live here) and the new reify-builtins registry, which holds only 7 rows. The migration tasks (τ3/τ8/τ9) are deferred. (3) The reify-compiler crate root flattens 325 items from 29 modules, and 8 of this slice's files import that root with `use super::*`, so none of them can be read in isolation. (4) A registered invariant is enforced opt-in: INV-EVAL-2's required-args cell_eval_ctx has 11 callers, while 21 production sites still hand-build the optional builder, 20 of them without containment. (5) Several docstrings in the slice now say false things: Snapshot is described as "immutable" with read-boundary revalidation, AmbientDefaults says purpose defaults are "never injected", and the disjointness pointers in orientation/joint_signatures and the expr.rs precedence comment are stale. Status of the INV-EVAL leads today: INV-EVAL-2 is type-enforced only where callers opt in (finding 2). INV-EVAL-4 is test-enforced (check_snapshot_cache_divergence, driven by harness_cache/snapshot_cache_divergence_gate.rs and the corpus sweep). gating.rs is still retained dead code, cited to #5023 via G-allow. The reify-syntax `pub use reify_ast::*` shim is still present but has zero code dependents (finding 10). All runtime claims were probed with /home/leo/src/reify/target/debug/reify, with REIFY_CACHE_DIR redirected to the seat scratch dir.

### constraints-and-geometry

**constraints-and-geometry/constraints-solver-kernel-gmsh-kernel-manifold-eval-topology-attribute-p** — The slice is two layers. The low layer is healthy where it is narrow. reify-kernel-gmsh's init.rs has a typed GmshGuard witness, one process lock and one refusal point. The watertightness preflight and the always-weld path in mesh_boundary.rs fail closed with a structured MeshContractViolation. cpsat.rs and topology_attribute_propagation.rs have clean layering: their production regions do not reference each other. The conformance crate holds the INV-GEO-2 producer x consumer matrix and is red-on-revert. The cost sits in three places. (1) reify-eval's constraint check path (engine_constraints.rs) is an impl-Engine satellite that touches 16 private Engine fields. It decides constraint identity, kernel identity and guard activity through strings and duplicated policies, and its geometric verdicts depend on Engine state that only earlier build/tessellate calls write. (2) reify-constraints/solver.rs holds 4686 production lines covering nine concerns, with a real floor<->core call cycle. Its 143 embedded tests exercise 34 of its 70 private items, and it carries 105 commits since 2026-07-06. 24 of those commits only correct its own prose. (3) Production prose share runs 42-72% across the slice (dispatcher 72%, init 69%, kernel_real 62%, cpsat 61%, solver 52%, manifold kernel 51%). Measured drift: every cross-file line cite in manifold kernel.rs points at the wrong code; the manifold module doc describes a stub replaced on 2026-06-04; dispatcher's module doc says ReprKind has 4 variants, but it has 5. The prior gmsh h10 finding is gone. The next change is cheapest in the gmsh and cpsat leaves and most expensive in engine_constraints and solver.

**constraints-and-geometry/kernel-occt-lib-eval-geometry-ops-stdlib-geometry-eval-realization-cache** — Each of the three alarm-sized files is one big match dispatch with in-file tests on top. reify-kernel-occt/src/lib.rs has 15,244 lines. 10,013 of them (65.7%, 244 tests) are an embedded #[cfg(test)] module; the sheet's embedded_test_lines=0 is wrong. The production half is 5,231 lines with 34.4% prose. OcctKernel::execute is one 1,293-line function with 55 GeometryOp arms. Each arm does four things inline: value extraction, validation, the FFI call, and BRepKind stamping. The arms exit through two protocols: an early return with an explicit or classified repr, or a fall-through to a default tail that stamps Solid. query is a 390-line match whose point, vector and bbox answers are JSON strings. So the expensive next change in this slice is "add or alter an op or a query". The agent must pick a repr policy that nothing tells it it has to pick, copy validation that already exists elsewhere in the file, and emit a string format that 11 parsers in 5 files depend on. Fix history says the same: 43 of 110 lib.rs commits since 2026-07-06 are fix/amend, and repr classification has been patched arm-by-arm in four tasks (6402, 7054, 5377, 7211). crates/reify-eval/src/geometry_ops.rs (13,242 lines, 36.4% prose) has improved since the hotspot survey. Geometry-op compilation is now enum-keyed (PrimitiveKind..ProfileKind tables with lock_dispatch_table plus a uniqueness test), so the string registration for constructors is gone. Selector dispatch is still string-keyed. Its 34,226-line child tests.rs calls 66 non-public functions directly. crates/reify-stdlib/src/geometry.rs has 2,615 production lines and 7,290 test lines. Its eval_geometry/diagnose pair dispatches the same names twice. crates/reify-eval/src/realization_cache.rs is healthy: the intermediate-key grammar has one home (intermediate_key), family eviction runs through a structured owner index and never parses a key, and the invariant is debug-checked at every removal. The fault-injection seams in the kernel (export_step_with_injected_fault_for_test, step_guard_probe_for_test, revolve_synthesis_post_sort_for_test) are test-fixtures-gated and documented as the only route to the guard's refusal arms. I judge them an acceptable test seam, not a finding. The conformance crate (reify-kernel-conformance) exists with OCCT/gmsh/manifold conformance binaries; I did not audit whether it is the single home of INV-GEO-1..4.

**constraints-and-geometry/kernel-openvdb-eval-topology-selectors-kernel-occt-handle-eval-tolerance** — The slice is a set of leaf modules that are individually careful (typed handles, exhaustive matches, explicit invariants in sketch.rs; a single send_request_blocking helper in handle.rs; a shared RepresentationWithin matcher in tolerance_combine) but whose next-change cost sits in three places. (1) Contracts that cross crates are carried as strings: kernel query replies are JSON text re-tokenized by four independent parsers in reify-eval, relation and joint kinds are bare names matched in compiler, constraints and eval, and realization identity is a string key with two grammars. (2) Rules are stated twice and kept in sync by comments and debug_asserts rather than by construction: sketch slot kinds (validator vs emitter), selector set composition (live vs carried-topology resolver), OCCT op lowering (execute vs the actor's execute_with_history dispatch, which skips the units tripwire), and trait conformance (eval copy of a now-public compiler function). (3) Prose: production halves run 48-79% comment lines in relate_solve, warm_pool, sweep_classifier, tolerance_combine and structural_classifier, much of it reviewer-answering history, and the fix log for relate_solve is dominated by prose repair. Ten of the thirteen size alarms are produced by embedded #[cfg(test)] modules over production halves of 292-971 lines; only handle.rs (1999 production lines of actor boilerplate) and solvespace.rs (1897) are large in production code, and geometry_ops/tests.rs is a 34,226-line single test module. No prior open findings were assigned to this slice.

**constraints-and-geometry/stdlib-sweep-constraints-relate-solve-constraints-registry-kernel-fidget** — The slice has two healthy cores and several costly seams. Healthy: dependent_reads.rs is a clean bottom layer (IR-only deps, one analysis shared by decomposition, fold filter and bound guard, iterative DFS, typed CellReads lookup). relate_solve.rs's residual model now carries units (ResidualRow/ResidualUnit) and RelateTolerance derives its rungs from one base length. registry_dispatch.rs is a real exhaustiveness mechanism (no `_` arm over EvalBuiltinId). realization_staleness.rs is mostly pure functions over the graph. Where the next-change cost sits: (1) structured values cross module and crate seams as strings: kernel query results are JSON text parsed by a hand-rolled comma-split in reify-eval, relation and joint kinds are matched as string literals at five or more sites each, and GeomRef::Sub operands are told apart by '.'. (2) Prose is doing the work of mechanism. registry.rs's non-test code is 52% comment lines, and solve_inner alone is 58%, much of it task-history and review-round narrative. decompose.rs's domain_of_auto has a 140-line doc whose 'PRECONDITION on the Logical slot' admits a dependency inversion enforced by nothing; that one is owned by pending #6681, so it is not a finding here. SingleKernelHolder's INVARIANT comment promises a parity test that covers 18 of 27 trait methods. (3) Kernel contract enforcement is not uniform. The shared assert_kernel_contract! real arm is instantiated only for OCCT; Fidget and Manifold carry bespoke Send+Sync pins; the OCCT stub cfg, with its contract suite, is compiled by no gate step now that OCCT presence is fatal. Also seen but not raised: reify-constraints/lib.rs keeps a loop-closure `pub use` re-export shim 'so downstream callers compile unchanged' (heuristic 13). Measured callers: 5 test files use the shim path and 2 use the canonical reify_stdlib path. No file in the slice is at or over 2000 lines; sweep.rs (1853) is 72% embedded tests.

### kinematics-and-motion

**kinematics-and-motion/stdlib-trajectory-stdlib-joints-stdlib-flexures-stdlib-snapshot-stdlib-o** — Read in full the PRODUCTION halves of the four ranked files (loop_closure_solver.rs 1-1406, joints.rs 1-1818, snapshot.rs 1-1700, tots.rs 1-960). I did not read the embedded test modules end to end; I read only the sites the findings cite. I followed seams into loop_closure.rs (value_for_joint, extract_loop_closure_chains, resolve_joint_value, strip_world_sentinel), loop_closure_value.rs (JointKind), input_shape.rs/trampoline.rs (the two TOTS arms), sweep.rs::build_snapshot_list, flexures/mod.rs and engine_eval's singularity scan. The slice is a stack of numerically careful leaf code (LDLᵀ, Powell-BFGS, KKT, FK walk) sitting on a weak shared data model. A joint is an untyped `Value::Map` with a string `kind`, and every consumer re-parses it. The project already has a typed `JointKind`/`JointValue`, but they are used only inside the Newton state, never as a parse boundary. So the cost of the next change is not in any one file. It is in keeping parallel per-kind tables in sync across joints/loop_closure/loop_closure_value/loop_closure_solver/snapshot/sweep/dynamics and the compiler. About 26 test loops over JOINT_KINDS try to hold them in line. KCC-γ (the multi-DOF widening) shows what that costs: the solver, value_for_joint and joint_range_midpoint were widened, but snapshot's duplicated copies of the same policies were not. The result is a live warm-start bug and an open-chain/closed-chain divergence that a test now pins. Eleven doc sites still describe the pre-widening world. The house `eval_*` dispatchers (eval_joints, eval_orientation, eval_snapshot) are big but mostly flat validate-then-construct arms, and on their own they are not where a new kind costs (heuristic 2/4 is tolerable there). Flexures is healthy: mod.rs is a narrow or_else dispatcher over ~1-1.5k-line siblings that share common.rs helpers, which have real sibling consumers. Production prose runs 37-52% in four of the five size-alarm files and is mostly task-step narration and defences answering a reviewer (Comments stance). All five size alarms are mostly embedded tests (joints 77%, orientation 78%, snapshot 70%, trampoline 60%, loop_closure_solver 57% of raw lines).

**kinematics-and-motion/stdlib-loop-closure-stdlib-mechanism-gcode-eval-trajectory-ops** — This slice has two halves in very different shape. reify-gcode (847 lines) is healthy. It has a semantic typed AST, one parser per dialect with Klipper delegating to the shared Marlin dispatch, Display as the round-trip inverse, and integration tests that drive only the public parse/display seams. Its only costs are prose pointers into an untracked plan and one sentinel char in a typed error. The kinematics half costs more. loop_closure.rs (968 production lines plus a 1,899-line test module) and mechanism.rs (757 plus 1,696) are each coherent alone: pure value-level functions, immutable Map-in/Map-out, and tests that mostly go through public functions or eval_builtin. The cost of the next change sits in the seams between these files and their siblings. First, the per-joint-kind encoding of JointValue to and from a dimensioned Value is matched on raw kind strings in five functions across loop_closure.rs and snapshot.rs, and the copies have already drifted. Second, the mechanism and loop-closure record is a string-keyed Value::Map whose edge class (parent-conflict vs cycle) and whose closing bodies are erased at construction. Seven files then re-derive them: by list contents, by a positional 'closing bodies are last' assumption, or by a hardcoded error discriminator. Third, production prose is about 50% of the lines in both files and is history-heavy; 7 of the 23 commits in task 7186's burst on these files were comment corrections. trajectory_ops.rs is a small, correct ComputeNode trampoline pair. Its headline helper, though, is dead code that its own docs and an audit pin describe as wired. No prior findings were open at this area.

### analysis

**analysis/eval-compute-targets-eval-modal-ops-solver-elastic-lib-solver-elastic-ev** — Read in full: the production half of both size-alarm files — modal_ops.rs lines 1-4547 (the #[cfg(test)] module, 8,288 lines, was indexed and its import list and assertion patterns sampled) and elastic_static.rs lines 1-5800 (the 9,538-line test module was indexed and its call sites sampled). Neither file counts as read fully, because most of each file's lines are tests. I followed seams only far enough to anchor claims: buckling.rs's readers, reify_ir::arg_acceptance, solver_progress and the engine's cancel wiring, assembly::test_support, and the .ri result declarations. The numerics are careful and heavily tested. The next-change cost is in how the trampolines talk to everything around them. (1) Each trampoline decodes stdlib Values (ElasticMaterial, *Options, loads) with its own ad-hoc readers, and failure policy varies by file: Result in elastic_static, panic! in buckling, silent 0.0 in modal_ops. The canonical dimension-checked reader (reify_ir::arg_acceptance) is used at only a few positions. (2) Result structs (ElasticResult, ModalResult, Mode) are spelled out field by field at several producer sites, and the Rust producers write fields the .ri declarations do not declare. (3) Cancellation has two channels, and the trampolines split between them. (4) elastic_static's single 1,170-line trampoline function handles five independent axes inline. modal_ops.rs bundles four compute targets plus a face-name BC-realization subsystem under a module doc that describes only one of them. About 46-47% of the production lines in both files are comments, and much of that prose answers reviewers. I measured drift in several of those comments (stale mesh sizes, a deleted function cited as the 'mirror', '10 args' over a 14-parameter function, '6 fields' over 7). In-file tests import 47 names, 30 of them fully private, so the tests pin the module's internal factoring.

**analysis/solver-elastic-elements-eval-engine-compute-solver-elastic-assembly-fdm** — The slice's numeric kernels are mostly carefully written, and where an axis of variability was named it got one mechanism. eigensolve.rs's PencilOperator trait puts the B=cI and B!=cI Lanczos operators behind one dispatch, and its C2/C3/C5 rules are single named helpers. shell_assembly's membrane, bending and rotation helpers are shared on purpose. The toolpath parser is a small, legible state machine. engine_compute single-sources its missing-trampoline diagnostic. In this slice the next change gets expensive in four places. (1) Force-density form-finding has no single home for its input invariants. Three anchored entry points plus form_find_free each re-run the sign, dimension, index and anchor checks, and fix 6563 had to patch the same guard three times. (2) Shell element primitives (ShellFrame, build_shell_frame, plane_stress_d, rotate_blockdiag) sit in the assembly-level shell_assembly.rs, so elements/, geometric_stiffness/ and shell_kinematics import upward into it, which closes cycles. (3) Compute-target identity is a bare &str, so per-target policy (persist codec, significance opt-in, fold hook, upstream insertion) lives in string-keyed tables across five reify-eval modules. (4) The solver-elastic integration tests copy one FEA harness about 69 times, and the copies have already drifted in behaviour. Every size-alarm src file is a 700-2000-line production unit carrying 1100-3900 lines of inline tests. Its production prose runs 39-65%, and part of that prose is history or measurement prose with dangling plan.json and stale file:line pointers. Healthy, with nothing filed: eigensolve's option/shape guards are duplicated in check_eigen_options_and_shapes vs check_lanczos_options, and its Krylov-window formula is written twice (run_partial_self_adjoint_eigen vs routes_to_dense_fallback). Both are low risk because the n=2..=128 boundary sweep catches drift. run_compute_dispatch repeats the restore-prior seed-then-donate block three times, which a helper would fix. No prior open findings were assigned to this area.

**analysis/shell-extract-mesh-morph-stdlib-dynamics-stdlib-fea-solver-elastic-solve** — The five big files are mostly embedded tests: production is 1280 lines of medial.rs, 471 of mesh-morph lib.rs, 1548 of dynamics/eval.rs, 1918 of fea.rs, 1235 of result.rs and 1071 of solver.rs. Tests make up 55-71% of each file. So the size alarms come from the test modules, not from code that needs splitting. Each production half breaks cleanly into downward-only groups (see h14). What makes the next change costly is three things.

(1) Logic that is copied instead of shared, and the copies have already drifted:
- the open-chain and closed-chain inverse-dynamics marshalling;
- the MassProperties Value shape, built and read in both reify-stdlib and reify-eval;
- the persistent-cache slab codecs in shell-extract and in reify-compute-contract;
- two definitions of 'same grid';
- the layout of SampledField's 3D data.

(2) Superseded API left exported next to its live replacement in mesh-morph: the morph() skeleton and a stats registry that is never written but is still served by a GUI RPC.

(3) Failure causes are rebuilt after the fact. Builtins return a bare Value::Undef, and six diagnose hooks re-run the validation in the same order to guess why.

Comment volume is high. Production prose share is 0.52 in medial.rs, 0.57 in mesh-morph lib.rs, 0.40 in dynamics/eval.rs and 0.55 in fea.rs. Much of it defends decisions to a reviewer, and every file:line citation I checked is stale.

Healthy parts:
- compose_morph's pipeline;
- walk_direction's WalkDirection value, which pairs the walk direction with its correction factor;
- the typed error enums in shell-extract (MedialError and GridValidationError);
- fea's TensorShape/TensorProjection factoring of the envelope family, which handles the projection axis with one mechanism.

No prior findings were open for this slice.

**analysis/solver-elastic-qoi-stdlib-modal-eval-compute-persist-solver-elastic-mpc** — The numeric kernels in this slice are individually well built: qoi.rs funnels both functionals through one private resolve chokepoint with typed errors and tests that drive only the public surface; volume_refine.rs gates every entry point through one tet_shape validator; sparse_util/interpolation/geometric_stiffness are small, coherent, layered leaf modules (geometric_stiffness/mod.rs is a facade over five element files, not a shim). Heuristic-13 metrics on the sheet mostly overstate: the flagged "reach-back" imports in membrane_load.rs and shell_result.rs are ordinary `crate::sibling::` imports and a `use super::*` in a test module, and the shell_result.rs deferred `use` of mitc3_plus inside shell_element_stress closes no cycle. The next-change cost sits elsewhere, in three places. (1) Seams that carry data as string-keyed Values: the shell-extract result has one encoder and three divergent decoders and a lossy persistence round trip; the persistent cache routes by target-name string in two match blocks plus three hand-kept lists; registry-free instances use two incompatible type_id conventions. (2) Copy-forks instead of extension: membrane_load.rs re-implements ~68% of tensegrity_load.rs's code lines. (3) Invariants held by pub-field structs or debug_assert on user data: MpcRow (validated constructor bypassable, pinned by a literal test), the 3x3 stress builtins (symmetry is a debug-only panic on .ri input), TetSpatialIndex/TetShape (tokens that do not pin the mesh or tolerance they were built for). Production regions run 37-59% comment lines; much is genuine contract documentation tied to PRDs, but file:line pointers (6 of 7 drifted) and plan-status prose (mpc T10 "pending", shell_result "T18-T20 will populate") have gone false. qoi.rs is the one size alarm, driven by an embedded 1,613-line test module that needs none of the file's private state. Fix-history density on qoi.rs (11) and compute_persist.rs (13) is TDD feature and review-amend traffic, not defect clusters; volume_refine.rs's amends are the TetShape gate hardening.

### tooling

**tooling/cli-main-lsp-references-lsp-diagnostics-lsp-server-lsp-analysis-doc-lsp** — The LSP slice is layered sensibly at the top (lib.rs -> server.rs handlers -> per-feature producers; blocking_work.rs is a small, deep, well-tested placement primitive; eval_guard has one predicate with one reporting form), and the big files are big mostly because of embedded #[cfg(test)] modules (server 2765, analysis 2761, diagnostics 3053, references 3739, goto_def 1809 test lines), all of which `use super::*` and so are not movable without a seam change. The next-change cost sits elsewhere: (1) the user-module front end (read -> module name -> parse_with_stdlib -> compile with checker -> module-path check -> guarded eval) has no single home and is spelled independently by the CLI (2), MCP context (3, using the no-prelude bootstrap `compile`), LSP diagnostics (2) and AnalysisContext (1); (2) per-Declaration-kind and per-template-cell questions are answered by many local matches/iterations (wildcard-terminated member-bearing-kind matches x5-6, value_cells+guarded chains spelled 4 ways and omitted twice), which is how purpose-nested structures and guarded members keep getting taught to one surface at a time; (3) constraint identity crosses the engine/LSP/CLI boundary only inside diagnostic message prose, so both front ends parse or reconstruct it; (4) compute_diagnostics_with_state (486 lines) and cmd_check (544 lines, two divergent engine-routing branches) are single functions that every diagnostics/check change must read whole; (5) production prose is 39-78% of non-blank lines in the LSP files and carries reviewer-answering rationale and demonstrably stale file:line pointers. reify-doc tests use public API only; their size is mirrored per-formatter suites, not a seam problem.

**tooling/config-lsp-hover-mcp-doc-build-lsp-convert-lsp-bridge-lsp-document-cli-d** — The slice is nine small-to-medium modules with no size alarm and no reach-back or cycle-closing imports. reify-config/cache.rs is a pure, well-typed resolver (typed source enums, strict serde). reify-lsp/document.rs puts its parse cache at document-version scope by construction. test_fixtures.rs is a good per-kind fixture table. reify-doc-build/cross_refs.rs is small and does one thing. The next-change cost is concentrated at the seams where a definition is enumerated by hand in several places. (1) The MCP tool contracts live inline in closures in reify-mcp/tools/*.rs. The GUI debug server therefore re-implements five tools under the same names, and the reify_set_parameter `value` grammar now means opposite things on the CLI and GUI surfaces. (2) The in-process LSP routes on method-name strings, and that set is spelled in five places across two crates plus the TS client. (3) Hover is one 212-line, CCN-36 function. It ends in a wildcard over Declaration and is not a consumer of the per-kind fixture table its siblings loop over. (4) MCP wire types carry enum-valued facts as free strings whose vocabulary each host invents. (5) The language-chunk topic registry is a triple enumeration, and another crate keeps it consistent by text-parsing this file's source. reify-config's Manifest has no production loader, while its rustdoc says the opposite. The Tests stance is mostly respected: the reaching items are cfg(test) helpers (peek_cached_parse observes cache sharing; occurrences/parse_one_clean are shared fixtures), and is_ident_byte is a real production pub(crate) predicate, not a test hook. None rose to a finding.

### reify-audit

**reify-audit** — reify-audit is a single crate: thirteen detectors as peer modules under a crate root (lib.rs). The root holds the shared vocabulary (Finding, Pattern, EvidenceRef, AuditContext), both side-effect seams (GitOps with RealGitOps and MockGitOps, JCodemunchOps with its Noop and Mock impls), and the crate's shared predicates. Every detector reaches its types through `use crate::{...}`, which is the reach-back the import graph records for 14 modules. Some structure is good. The bin's DETECTORS table puts selection, jcodemunch need and corpus refusal on one row per detector (heuristic 3 done right). PDOCCOVER's Disposition and DegenerateInputs enums and PDIAG's RatchetVerdict make verdicts typed. task_rows.rs shares the task-DB scaffolding properly. The next-change cost sits in four places:
(a) The inter-module protocol is still stringly. A finding's kind and line travel inside `summary` and are parsed back by the merge-gate fingerprint, the /audit skill and the tests.
(b) Recognisers for Rust source shapes (cfg(test), test paths, allow(dead_code), G-allow) are re-implemented per detector, and the copies have different semantics.
(c) Failure handling at the git and task-DB seams is decided per caller rather than by type.
(d) Production prose share is 52-59% in five files. The prose is mostly measurements, rejected alternatives and test-name pins, and it has measurably drifted from the code, including the claimed severity of the merge-gated PTODO lane and a `--since` flag that changes nothing.
Seven files exceed 2,000 lines. In four of them (pdiag, pdoccover, ptodo, jcodemunch_client) much of the bulk is an embedded `mod tests` of 1,576-2,515 lines. In five (lib, p5_phantom_done, pdiag, pdoccover, ptodo) the production region is mostly prose. The hotspot-program fixes visible here are scan_util (#6036), task_rows, read_relative and NoopJCodemunchOps moving into lib. They removed specific copies, but no shared seam yet exists for the four themes above.

### gui

**gui/gui-tauri-src-gui-viewport-gui-panels-gui-editor** — This slice centres on two Rust files. engine.rs (9,588 lines, 49.6% comment lines) holds EngineSession plus about ten unrelated pure-function groups: Value-to-DTO projection, display routing and appearance, tensegrity extraction, mechanism and joint descriptors, the entity tree, value-string and unit parsing, atomic file writes, line/col mapping, FEA field sampling, and the startup cache sweep. None of those groups depends on EngineSession. Tests reach the file through 28 `_for_test` helpers (about 230 call sites) and about 20 pub(crate) free functions. debug_server.rs (5,530 lines) holds a 1,267-line tool registry, the JSON-RPC transport, string-keyed dispatch, the five AI write tools, and a 2,268-line embedded test module.

Since the 2026-07 hotspot pass, real mechanisms exist. The `gui_state!` macro forces every GuiState field to declare its sync channel at compile time. `post_engine_call_telemetry` is the one emit call site. `CoreState` type-enforces the core-field invariant. The debug write tools refresh the shared baseline through two named seams.

The next-change cost now comes from four places:
- **Duplication kept consistent by prose.** GuiState is assembled by 4 hand-written literals, and the reload pipeline is open-coded in 4 entry points. Both have measurably drifted: the FEA case switch drops display panes and appearance, and load_from_compiled misses a cache clear.
- **An eight-sink event axis spread across files**, enforced only by per-entry-point tests.
- **Debug-tool identity spread over 5–6 cross-language lists**, held together by regex scans of Rust source.
- **Prose volume that drifts.** 9 of 16 line-number pointers are stale and 3 doc blocks are attached to the wrong function.

Re-measured fix history since 2026-07-06: engine.rs has 58 fix/amend commits out of 110, debug_server.rs 39 of 95. Coverage caveat: engine.rs was read in full except its 188 embedded test lines. debug_server.rs was read for lines 1–140 and 1324–3420, plus spot-reads of tool_defs; its test module past line 3420 was not read. The five large test files were measured (prose share, sections, test counts) and sampled, not read.

**gui/gui-stores-gui-root-gui-debug-gui-other-gui-hooks-gui-components-gui-uti** — The slice is a SolidJS app whose composition root (App.tsx, 2395 lines; the App component alone is lines 442-2392) wires ~15 stores, 11 IPC subscriptions, side-panel layout math and every panel inline, and imports 32 names straight from the IPC module ./bridge (73 exports). The stores themselves are mostly healthy: small factories over createStore with narrow returned surfaces (editorStore, mechanismStore with injected deps, claudeStore with a discriminated OutboundMessage union, layoutStore, feaModeStoreRegistry, viewportStore); ipc.ts, shortcuts.ts, useKeyboardShortcuts.ts and the vitest flake reporter are clean, pure, and well-factored. Next-change cost concentrates in four places: (1) App.tsx, where facts are restated between a JSX child list and a hand-built grid-template-rows string (already wrong for two panels), subscription lifecycles are hand-listed three times, and testability is bought by 9 module-level vi.mocks plus a 561-line source-parsing guard over the mocks; (2) debug/bridge.ts, one 1532-line buildHandlers closure of 58 handlers whose parameter validation is hand-rolled per handler (12 guard copies censused in a 60-line comment) although the Rust ToolDef already declares each schema, with Rust/TS registry parity enforced by regex over debug_server.rs; (3) the Rust<->TS wire contract in types.ts, hand-mirrored with no codegen, enum-valued fields typed as string, and parity pinned by per-field regex-over-Rust-source tests; (4) frontend re-derivations of backend facts (quantity grammar in unitLadder.ts, geometry-ness by regex over a Type Display string in autoViewGenerator.ts). Prose is heavy and often reviewer-answering (task history, rejected alternatives, line-number citations that have already drifted: bridge.ts:618/620/656/699 and App.tsx:810 point at the wrong lines today). No prior open findings existed for this area.
