### cross-area

**cross:spot** — Read-only at a030ee7528 (worktree review-all-reify-20261010). Read docs/invariants.md (24 rows) and docs/legibility/design-invariants.md (census table plus INV-PD-1/2). For each registry row I checked whether the named mechanism exists in the tree: cell_commit.rs, cell_eval_ctx.rs, detectors.rs, cache_divergence.rs, invariants.rs, engine_compute catch_unwind, test_nan_safe_ordering_guard_wired.sh, gui_state_schema.rs, the assert_kernel_contract! arms, registry_drift_tests.rs. I also counted the files that cite each INV id, and compared reify-audit's bin pattern list against the census table. Scripts in the scratch dir did the following: (1) parsed all 29 reify-compiler `*_NAMES` slices (352 names, 365 entries), the reify-builtins rows (7) and reify-stdlib `"name" =>` dispatch arms (218), then took set differences; (2) diffed the `@optimized("…")` targets in stdlib .ri (16) against the string literals in register_compute_fns (18), plus the LSP hand list; (3) matched Rust `type_name:` mints against stdlib `structure def` names; (4) listed every `fn …(v: &Value) -> Option<f64>` helper across crates and compared each body with reify-ir Value::as_f64 (312 production call sites); (5) for ElasticOptions, BucklingOptions and ModalOptions, counted production string reads of each declared param and compared the .ri defaults with the Rust fallbacks and solver Default impls; (6) listed every DEPTH/NESTING const, found the lint-before-compile order in reify-compiler lib.rs:535-536, and probed with /home/leo/src/reify/target/debug/reify (main 469af2231a; a030ee7528 is an ancestor and the four relevant files are unchanged) using `reify check` on generated 200- and 300-deep `(1 + …)` expressions; (7) compared the Diagnostic->DiagnosticInfo projections in gui engine.rs, reify-cli mcp_context.rs and reify-lsp convert.rs; (8) traced support-kind handling across fea_multi_case.ri, reify-stdlib supports.rs, modal_ops.rs, elastic_static.rs, eval shell_solve.rs and solver shell_boundary.rs/shell_solve.rs; (9) compared the KernelId registry-name API with module_pragmas KNOWN_V02_KERNELS and the engine_build/dispatcher bridges; (10) diffed the Rust wire structs in gui/src-tauri types.rs and the gui_state! field list against the gui/src/types.ts interfaces (27 pairs, 136 fields), and checked the delta event names against bridge.ts. I checked the verify-pipeline manifests (verify-pipeline-infra-tests.txt vs run-all-classification.manifest); they answer different questions, so I raised no new finding there. I dropped two other candidates as not SPOT: the three task-citation grammars answer different questions, and the Poisson .ri bound is deliberately stricter than the Rust check. I did not repeat costs already in the canonical set; where I had new evidence for one of them I recorded it as a re-observation.

**cross:layering** — Tree a030ee7528, read only. (1) Read import_graph.json with a script. It has 3,981 edges, 145 reach-backs, 1 cycle, 1 hidden cycle and 1 closes_cycle deferred import (all three in gui TS). It records no Rust cycles because it does not follow inline crate::X paths. (2) Built the workspace crate DAG from `cargo metadata --no-deps` (36 packages), separating normal/build edges from dev edges. Computed transitive closures and longest-path levels, then checked each surprising edge by grepping how the depending crate uses the dependency in production code: contract→solver-elastic, solver-elastic→kernel-gmsh, kernel-occt→(opt) mesh-morph→eval, audit→test-support→eval, expr→fdm, constraints→stdlib, cli/gui→reify-geometry. (3) Wrote modgraph.py, which builds top-level-module graphs for 10 crates from production text (`crate::<mod>` and `crate::{..}` lists, comment lines dropped, cut at the first top-level cfg(test)), and ran Tarjan SCC on each. Results: reify-eval has a 24-module SCC (95,192 raw lines) plus a 3-module one; reify-ir {geometry,value,expr,boundary_attachment} and {completeness,ranked}; reify-stdlib 4; reify-solver-elastic 4. I then listed and read every upward edge that closes the reify-eval SCC. (4) Counted Engine assembly and kernel-registry sites per front end with a brace-matched cfg(test)-stripping filter (prod.py), and read kernel_registry pick logic, inventory anchors and the reify-eval Cargo.toml constraints. (5) Measured the split of each analysis target across reify-stdlib *trampoline.rs halves and reify-eval *_ops/compute_targets, and which reify-eval files use each dependency crate. (6) Read the declared layer model (.jcodemunch.jsonc layers, scripts/assert-crate-dag.sh B1–B6) and compared its coverage with the inversions measured above. (7) Read task statuses through fused-memory: #6356 pending, #4934 done, #8289 pending, #2947 cancelled, #4744 done. Not raised as findings: solver-elastic→kernel-gmsh and solver-hosted meshing, because the active meshing-service PRD (#8289 ζ, BT20 cargo-tree check) owns them; Engine impl spread, the reify-compiler glob namespace, SingleKernelHolder, the shell_assembly cycle and the snapshot↔dynamics cycle, because they are already canonical (re-observed below). reify-runtime (2,289 lines, one CLI consumer in dev.rs) was noted but not measured deeply enough to raise. Intermediates are in cross/layering/ (meta.json, crate_deps.json, modgraph.py, prod.py, *.edges.json).

**cross:dimensions** — Read-only at a030ee7528 (worktree review-all-reify-20261010). I wrote a production-only grep helper (prod_grep.py: tracked crates/**/*.rs and gui/src-tauri/src/*.rs; skips tests/ dirs and *tests.rs; stops each file at the first top-level #[cfg(test)] mod; drops // lines) and a brace-matched function-similarity helper (fnsim.py, difflib over stripped non-comment lines). Both are in the scratchpad under cross/dimensions/. Each candidate axis in the brief was measured as follows.

(1) REIFY_BUILD_SCHEDULER: grepped BuildScheduler and unified_pass* uses in engine_build.rs production code (lines 1-13987). Found 10 decision sites across the 5 build surfaces. Dated the default flip with git log -S. Counted files that pin the legacy arm.

(2) Kernel selection and capability gating: located the dispatcher::dispatch callers (3) and every geometry_kernels/kernels .get/.get_mut lookup in reify-eval (26), and classified each by its key: default_kernel_name, handle-recorded kernel, plan source name, or hard-coded KernelId. Counted default_kernel_name references (74 in 7 files) and CapabilityDescriptor.supports callers (2, both in dispatcher.rs). Kernel-name string literals turned out to be contained (19 sites, 10 of them in reify-core kernel.rs), so that part is not a finding.

(3) Guard knobs: read hooks/main-gate-lib.sh. gate_switch_on is one shared mechanism for the four hook knobs (enforce/bypass for main-gate and stash-guard), so the hooks are not a finding. The *_DISABLE/SKIP knobs in scripts use a uniform `= 1` truthiness test (7 of 9 spellings identical). That left the pre-done gate's two opposite-polarity knobs, which I traced through scripts/reify-audit-freshness.sh, deploy-reify-audit-predone-hook.sh and p5_phantom_done.rs.

(4) verify.sh profile axis: grepped PROFILE, _profile and rel comparisons and the *_DEBUG/*_RELEASE variable pairs (non-comment lines), and mapped each site to its function.

(5) GUI paths: listed the debug_server.rs production fns (before line 3261) that call run_on_engine/with_engine_lock (13 sites: 6 writes, 7 reads). Counted EvalRequest constructions and queue submissions in commands.rs, main.rs and mcp_context.rs. Read eval_queue.rs's module doc and the serial-debug-ops prose.

(6) Engine configuration/posture: counted Engine constructions per front end (CLI 9, CLI-MCP 1, LSP 4, GUI 3). Checked each for compute registration, persistent cache, repr-tol capture and openvdb ensure. Traced env reads inside reify-eval (BuildScheduler::from_env, WarmStatePool::from_env_or_default / from_config_or_env, long_chain_threshold_from_env ×4, PATH in fdm_slice). Diffed the persistent-cache resolver inputs in reify-cli cache.rs and gui engine.rs.

(7) Solver execution mode: grepped resolve_execution_modes callers (1) and inline SolverMode/AssemblyMode constructions (30 lines in 9 files).

(8) eval vs eval_cached: fnsim gave 85 lines matched in runs of 6 or more, ratio 0.199. Compared the sets of called names (94 vs 63, 40 shared) and listed the post-pass call sites in each. The result is reported as a re-observation.

Native-dep cfg(has_*) gating was also measured and is not a finding: 856 cfg lines, but only 2 production sites outside the reify-kernel-* crates. I checked every finding against the 300+ canonical ids. Overlaps are recorded as re_observations or named in the statement. Lizard was not run, so no CCN figures are claimed.

**cross:stateless** — Read-only, at a030ee7528 in the review worktree. I read the import graph (3981 edges, 1 cycle, 1 hidden cycle, both in the GUI TS layer) with a script. Because that instrument does not see Rust impl-block or field coupling, I measured state coupling directly with git grep and awk, excluding tests/ directories, *_tests.rs files and the post-#[cfg(test)] regions of the main files.

(1) Engine per-build tables. Read reset_per_build_state (engine_build.rs 3267). For 9 side-table fields I counted the self.<field> readers per file. I traced achieved_repr_tol (written by the tessellate surfaces, read in engine_constraints.rs dispatch_constraints) and realization_handles (written by the Build surfaces, read by measure_dfm_rules and measure_gdt_conformance) to every non-test Engine::check caller in the CLI, LSP and GUI (6). I compared each caller's preceding calls (set_capture_repr_tol, realize_for_check, tessellate_realizations) and read reset_geometry_for_reload and its single GUI call site.

(2) Capture toggles and last_* outputs. Listed the Engine last_* getters and their gating, and counted the external consumers of the ungated ones (6 GUI sites). Checked which call writes and which clears last_eval_set and last_demand_prune_measurement. Traced trace_undef_causes: its origins are written only in engine_eval.rs; engine_edit.rs has 0 references to them. The GUI path that consumes them is preview_parameter → edit_check → build_gui_state → build_values. Read the GUI dispatched_entities proxy in surface_geometry_derived_cells.

(3) Ambient and global state census. Production thread_local! blocks: 6. Mutable or env-cached statics: about 15. Followed up the semantic ones: solver_progress, the manifold MeshContractMode OnceLock (against Engine::mesh_contract_mode and set_mesh_contract_mode callers) and the mesh-morph COUNTERS (5 consumer files across 3 crates).

(4) Compute-trampoline registration. Counted register_production_compute_fns call sites (CLI 2 through a helper, GUI 1, test_runner 1) against Engine constructions in the CLI, LSP and GUI.

(5) Snapshot entry points. Counted module-identity checks in check_snapshot, build_snapshot and tessellate_snapshot (0, 0, 0). Read the Snapshot fields and the LSP last_content_hash mirror.

(6) Warm-lane flock protocol. Found 6 <lane>.lock path derivations across 5 scripts, using FD numbers 9 and 8 and a by-path probe. Read the --assume-lane-lock-held branch (0 verification).

(7) Test seams. Counted test-feature cfg sites per crate and cross-crate dev-dep feature enablements (6). Counted vi.mock targets across the 81 GUI test files and the production TS modules that import Tauri IPC directly (6 besides bridge.ts).

(8) DF_VERIFY_ROLE. Counted readers (56 files) and found the run_all.sh snapshot-and-rewrite.

Of these candidates, 7 became findings and 7 became re-observations of existing canonicals. The rest were dropped: compiler recursion depth thread-local (benign), the stdlib OnceLock caches (immutable), and the length-tripwire thread-local (test arm only).

- remedy: Group Engine's 67 fields (f263: 15 files read them as self.<field>) into owned sub-structs by lifetime class: EngineConfig resolved once from env, manifest and front end; PerBuildState with reset(surface); EvalArtifacts holding the undef-cause map beside the snapshot it was classified from (f342); PurposeState as one HashMap<PurposeName, ActivePurpose> (f265); Caches; Instrumentation. One construc
- deletable: 28 cfg(test-instrumentation) accessors and 9 always-present counters on Engine (f269, f258); the 5 parallel purpose maps; last_* getters and the GUI mesh-key dispatch proxy; inventory link anchors and test_registry_override; the 699-line Engine struct doc; the soft-vs-hard no-trampoline inference in
- size L · risk high · mode spawn
- seam candidates: Engine constructor signature (reify-cli main.rs 9 sites, mcp_context, reify-lsp 4, gui 3, test_runner, 24+ test files via cache_store_mut), scripts/check-compute-trampoline-registration.sh KNOWN_SITES, docs/invariants.md INV-FEA-1 / INV-BUILD-1 rows
- open choice: Does the front end pass a posture enum into one constructor, or does each front end get a named constructor (batch-CLI, interactive-GUI, LSP-analysis)?
  - One constructor taking EngineConfig + Registration enum — trade-off: Every caller is edited once; posture is data the engine can print and the lint can read — long term: A new front end cannot forget a posture; a new knob is one EngineConfig field
  - Named posture constructors — trade-off: Smaller diff; the postures stay fixed sets — long term: A fourth posture needs a new constructor and the lint table grows; postures can drift between constructors

Verdict: revise; synthesis revised: True. Checked against the digest file (synth_digest.json), the seats' full rows and skeptic notes in workflow-result.json, and the tree at /home/leo/src/reify/.claude/worktrees/review-all-reify-20261010 (a030ee75). Joins that hold: all 347 finding ids and the 13 open priors appear exactly once in the matrix; every weakened verdict is in the matrix (no weakened-hidden); all seven fixed priors (p1 p5 p6 p9 p11 p15 p19) carry gone_verdict=confirmed in their seat's prior_verdicts and none is ranked or streamed (no unverified-fixed); the f65 re-verification is correct (shared `Ok(self.store(shape))` tail at reify-kernel-occt/src/lib.rs:3903; `store` defaults to BRepKind::Solid at :809). Mechanisms I confirmed absent, so their streams are not duplicates: INV-META-1 checker (docs/invariants.md row is 'proposed', reify-audit only references INV-SF-6), a line-cite ratchet (test_cited_test_paths_resolve.sh + cited-test-path-lib.sh resolve paths only), a module-SCC ratchet (assert-crate-dag.sh has no SCC pass), DiagnosticInfo::from_diagnostic, RelationKind, LspMethod, ts-rs/specta, verify.sh --print-role-walls/--print-gates, tree-sitter-generate.sh --check, seed-warm-lane.sh --capabilities, wallclock_rust_guard_lib.sh. call_compute_fn_guarded is a genuine dedupe (two catch_unwind sites in engine_compute.rs at 276 and 1097). tests/common/fea_harness.rs extends an existing tests/common/mod.rs that holds only tensegrity prism helpers, so not a duplicate. harness-layout-lib.sh exists and the run_all stream names it as the home (adoption, fine). KernelId already exists in reify-core/src/kernel.rs and the Engine-config stream treats it as the authority (fine). Items for the lead, not join errors: (a) the digest file marks f52-f57, f65-f68, f237-f239, f284 as 'unverified' (skeptic lost to a usage cap) while the inline index in the brief shows them weakened/confirmed; the synthesis evidently read the file for f65 (re-verified) but may have read 'confirmed' for f53/f57/f67/f68 - no row order changes under either reading, but the digest's observation.unchecked is wrongly empty for p20; (b) contradictions[f23,f326] says f23 is 'sequenced after f326 in the Compute-targets stream' but f23 is not among that stream's canonicals (it is in no stream) - add it; (c) f336 is a canonical of both Engine-configuration and Build-surfaces, and both deletables claim the same None-conflation sites; f175 is in both Joint-kind and Prose-rehoming - home each once with a pointer from the other; (d) analysis|h11 tie order under the formula also needs f26 (seat 3) before f40 (seat 4) at equal payoff; (e) the Stdlib-codecs seam 'SupportKind as the one support decode' is consistent with f321's skeptic only if it means the string decode, not the per-element support policy (beam vs tet vs shell differ by design) - say 'decode'. Verdict is revise: errors 1, 2, 6-13 change row orders or memberships; 16-21 change stream remedies or deletables.
